That’s where it’s really obvious, I guess, because there’s the forklift and the boxes and everybody’s trying to move fast, so when I do it there, it becomes a problem right away.\n\nLike I said, I’ll be near the forklift and just kind of stop with my arms up, like I’m stuck there, even though I know I shouldn’t be standing there. Or I’ll copy the beeping sounds it makes, or the hand signals the guys use, and I’ll do it more than once, not just like a joke. And the boxes… I’ll start moving them into these little patterns or lines instead of where they’re supposed to go, even while people are yelling at me to stop or saying I’m messing everything up.\n\nOutside of work, I don’t know… I’m not around forklifts or pallets of boxes the same way, so it doesn’t come up like that. But the reason I’m here is mostly because at the warehouse it keeps happening and they’re saying I can’t keep doing it there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to understand or talk to your coworkers when you're not doing these repetitive behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I’m not doing the box thing or the beeping thing, talking to them can get messed up. Like my supervisor will ask something simple, like which pallets are supposed to go on the truck, and I’ll try to answer, I really will, because I can see the load sheet and I know he needs the answer fast.\n\nBut then when I start explaining it, my words don’t stay on the pallets. I’ll be saying, “That one goes with the load sheet,” and then somehow I’m talking about my old house, because the paper reminds me of boxes there, or moving, and then I’ll jump to fishing, because there were coolers and crates, and then I’ll be saying something about church boxes or donation boxes, and I can tell by their faces they’re not following me anymore.\n\nIt’s frustrating because in my head it feels connected when it starts, like there’s a reason I’m saying it, but by the time I’m done, nobody knows which pallets I meant. Then they get irritated and ask me again, shorter, like “Left or right?” and I feel embarrassed because I was trying to answer the first time.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty making or keeping friends outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. I mean, it’s not like I have a big group of friends anyway, but it’s gotten harder to keep people around outside of work too.\n\nI used to have things where I’d see people, like at the church food-bank shifts, and that kind of counted for me, because you’re working next to people and talking a little, and after a while they know you. But I stopped showing up there because people started saying they couldn’t count on me, or that they didn’t feel like things were safe around me anymore. That really stung, because I wasn’t trying to be unreliable or dangerous or whatever. I just… I guess I wasn’t doing what they needed me to do.\n\nAnd at work, I’ve already lost the forklift duties, so that changed how people treat me too. Some guys used to talk to me more when I was doing that job, but now it’s like they don’t want to be put with me, or they keep checking on me like I’m going to mess something up. Then with school, I’ve fallen behind in my community-college classes, so I’m not really keeping up with people there either. I’ll miss things or be behind, and then it’s awkward to talk to classmates because they’re moving on and I’m still trying to catch up.\n\nSo, yeah… I don’t know if it’s “making friends” exactly, but keeping connections has gotten pretty bad. People don’t seem to trust me the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or distressed when someone stops you from making the patterns with the boxes or copying the sounds?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I do get upset, but it’s not always like I’m angry at them exactly. It’s more like everything gets interrupted and suddenly everybody’s looking at me, and I realize I’m doing it again.\n\nLike at the warehouse, if I’m making the boxes into those little lines or patterns, I can feel like I’m just trying to get them to sit right, like there’s a way they’re supposed to be arranged. And then someone yells, “Stop doing that,” or they grab a box back, and it kind of jars me. Same with the forklift stuff — I’ll freeze with my arms up near it, or start copying the beeping or the hand signals, and when someone cuts me off or tells me to knock it off, I feel embarrassed and tense.\n\nSometimes I want to explain that I’m not trying to mess everything up, but then it’s already a big scene because people are shouting and the work is stopped. So yeah, I feel distressed, mostly because I know they’re mad and I know I’m causing problems, but in the moment it’s hard to just… stop cleanly and act normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating any specific actions or behaviors when you're alone or not at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, sort of. It’s not only at work, but work is where people notice it the most because it slows everybody down and there are forklifts and boxes everywhere.\n\nLike, the reason they even made me come talk to somebody, I think, is because at the warehouse I keep doing these things that don’t really make sense to other people. I’ll freeze with my arms up near the forklift, like I’m stuck in the middle of a motion, or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds or the hand signals. And with the boxes, I’ll start moving them into these little patterns, like they need to line up a certain way, even if that’s not what I’m supposed to be doing.\n\nWhen I’m by myself, it can be more quiet, I guess. There’s nobody yelling, so it doesn’t turn into such a big thing. But I still notice myself wanting to repeat the same kind of movements or arrange things in a certain pattern. It’s hard to explain because it doesn’t feel like I’m deciding, “Okay, now I’m going to do this weird thing.” It just starts happening, and then I realize I’ve been doing it for a while. At work it gets worse because everyone’s shouting for me to stop, and that makes me more tense and embarrassed, and then I feel even more stuck.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the patterns you make with the boxes or the sounds you copy need to be symmetrical or perfect in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “symmetrical” is exactly the word I’d use. It’s more like… they look wrong if they’re just sitting there random, and then I start moving them into these little lines or shapes, like they’re supposed to go a certain way. I’m not standing there thinking, “This has to be perfect,” like a math thing. It’s more that I get caught in it.\n\nThat’s kind of why they brought me in, I guess. At the warehouse I’ll be near the forklift and I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping sounds or the hand signals, and with the boxes I’ll move them into strange little patterns even when people are yelling at me to stop. By then it’s not really about making something beautiful or perfectly even. It’s just like I’m stuck doing it, and everyone else is getting mad because I’m not doing the job right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the patterns you make or the sounds you copy have to be exactly right in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “exactly right” like I’m checking it against something, you know? It’s not like I have a picture in my head and I’m trying to match it perfectly. It’s more like I get pulled into it, and if it’s off, or if the boxes are just sitting there in a way that feels wrong, I start moving them around into these little patterns.\n\nThat’s kind of the whole problem at work. I guess they brought me in because at the warehouse I keep doing stuff that people say is weird or disruptive. Like I’ll be by the forklift and just sort of freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping sounds it makes, or copy the hand signals people are doing. And with the boxes, I’ll start making these strange little lines or patterns with them, even when everyone is yelling at me to stop.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d say it has to be perfect, exactly. It’s more like once it starts happening, I have a hard time breaking out of it, even when I know people are getting mad.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset when you're not allowed to finish one of your patterns or behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do get upset, but it’s not always like I’m mad because I “wanted to finish” it, like it was a project or something. It’s more like when somebody interrupts me, especially at work, it jolts me and makes everything worse. Like if I’m moving the boxes into those little patterns and someone starts yelling, “Stop doing that, get back to work,” I know they’re probably right, but in the moment it feels like my body doesn’t switch over fast enough.\n\nAnd then I get embarrassed, because everyone is looking at me, and then I’m more tense, and then sometimes I get more stuck instead of less. Same with the forklift stuff. I’ll be standing there with my arms up, kind of frozen, or I’ll catch myself copying the beeping sounds or the hand signals, and if someone cuts in and yells at me, I feel… I don’t know, cornered maybe. Not exactly angry at them, just upset and confused and like I can’t get myself to stop quickly enough.\n\nSo, yeah, I guess I do get upset when I’m not allowed to keep going, but it’s not because the pattern is important in a normal way. It’s more that stopping suddenly feels really hard, and then everybody yelling makes it feel even harder.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do things in a specific way or follow a certain routine in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a routine, like a normal routine where I have to brush my teeth a certain way or line things up before bed or anything like that. It’s not really my whole day being scheduled or me having rules for everything.\n\nIt’s more like… in the moment, especially at the warehouse, I get caught doing things in this certain way and I can’t shift out of it fast enough. Like I’ve said, I’ll be by the forklift and suddenly I’m just standing there with my arms up, kind of frozen, or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds it makes, or copying the hand signals people are doing. And with the boxes, instead of just moving them where they’re supposed to go, I’ll start arranging them into these odd little patterns or lines, even when everyone’s telling me to stop.\n\nSo it’s not like I wake up and think, “I have to follow this routine today.” It’s more like something grabs my attention and then I start doing it the same way over and over, and people get mad because I’m supposed to be working, not doing all that. I know it looks weird. I can tell from how they react. But when it’s happening, it feels really hard to just drop it and act normal right away.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these repetitive behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel like they're happening to you against your will?\n\nPatient: I don’t really feel like it’s “me,” if that makes sense. I mean, I know I’m the one doing it, and I can see my hands moving the boxes or hear myself copying the forklift beeps, so I’m not saying someone else is doing it. But it doesn’t feel like a choice the way normal choices feel.\n\nLike at the warehouse, I’ll be near the forklift and then I’m just… stuck there with my arms up, or I start copying the hand signals people are doing, or making the same beeping sound back. And with the boxes, I’m supposed to be moving them for work, but then I start putting them into these weird little lines or patterns instead. People are yelling at me to stop, and part of me knows I’m making everything worse, but I can’t just snap out of it fast enough.\n\nSo I guess it feels more like it’s happening to me, or like I get pulled into it. It’s embarrassing because it looks like I’m trying to be difficult, but I’m really not trying to make everyone mad. It just kind of takes over in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your behaviors have caused problems for you in your relationships or daily life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s caused problems. Not just at work, although work is the big one people notice first. I lost my forklift duties, which is… honestly that was a big deal, because that was one of the things I was trusted to do. Now they don’t want me around it the same way because they say it’s not safe, or they can’t count on me to keep moving and not get stuck doing something else. And I get why they say that, but it still feels bad, like I got pushed down to being less useful.\n\nOutside of work, it’s been happening too. I’ve fallen behind in my community-college classes because I’ll miss things or not keep up right, and once you fall behind it’s like every assignment makes the next one harder. I used to think I could catch up if I just had a quiet weekend or something, but then I don’t, and then people start asking why I’m not turning stuff in or why I’m not there.\n\nAnd at church, I stopped showing up for the food-bank shifts. I wasn’t fired or anything, not exactly, but it got to where I could tell people didn’t really trust me to be dependable. They’d say things like they need to know who’s actually going to be there, or they need things to be safe and organized, and I felt like they were saying it carefully because they didn’t want to be mean. So I just stopped going, because it was easier than showing up and feeling like everyone was watching to see if I messed something up.\n\nSo, yeah, it’s not just annoying habits or whatever. It’s messed with work, school, and even the things I was trying to do to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you repeat or the patterns you make have to be symmetrical or perfect in some way?\n\nPatient: Not really symmetrical, no. It’s not like I’m measuring the boxes or trying to make both sides match, like some perfect shape. I don’t look at it and think, “That corner is wrong” or “This has to be even.” It’s more… I just start moving them into these strange little patterns or lines, and then I keep going with it even when I’m supposed to stop.\n\nLike at the warehouse, I’ll be near the forklift and I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping sounds it makes, or copy hand signals someone is doing, and it’s the same kind of thing with the boxes. Something about the way they’re sitting there, or the movement, I guess, catches me, and then I’m arranging them instead of doing the actual job. People can be yelling at me to cut it out, and I still don’t switch back right away.\n\nSo, no, I wouldn’t say it has to be perfect. It’s not about making it beautiful or balanced. It’s just these odd patterns I get pulled into, and then everybody sees it and gets upset because I’m holding things up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you repeat or the patterns you make are driven by a need to make things orderly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s exactly about order, like not in the way people mean when they say they need things neat. I’m not trying to clean the warehouse up or make the boxes make sense. It’s more like I get caught in doing the thing.\n\nLike with the boxes, I’ll start moving them into these weird little lines or shapes, and I know I’m supposed to be doing the actual job, getting them where they’re supposed to go, but then I’m just… doing that instead. And people will be yelling, like, “Stop, leave them alone,” or telling me I’m holding things up, and I can hear them, but it takes me a while to break out of it.\n\nSame with the forklift stuff. I’ll be standing there with my arms up, or copying the beeping sounds, or copying somebody’s hand signals, and it’s not because I’m trying to make things orderly. It’s more like the movement or the sound grabs me and I start repeating it. I guess that’s why they brought me in, because it keeps happening and it looks strange and it causes problems, but in my head it doesn’t feel like, “I need this to be organized.” It feels more like I’m stuck in the behavior.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to control something when you repeat these behaviors or make these patterns?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m trying to control something, exactly. That’s the part that’s hard to explain, because people see me doing it and they think I must have some reason, like I’m trying to make the warehouse safer or fix the boxes or signal something, but it doesn’t feel like that inside.\n\nIt’s more like… I get pulled into it. Like by the forklift, I’ll just freeze with my arms up, and I know that looks ridiculous or maybe even dangerous, but in the moment I’m just there like that. Or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds, or if somebody is doing hand signals, I’ll copy those too, and it’s not like I’m thinking, “If I do this, then something will be under control.” I’m not making a plan. It just starts happening, and then once I’m in it, stopping feels slow, like my body didn’t get the message yet.\n\nSame with the boxes. I’ll start moving them into these strange little patterns, and everyone is yelling at me to stop because I’m holding everything up, and I can hear them, but it’s like I can’t switch gears right away. I’m not trying to control the boxes or control other people. If anything, it feels like I’m the one not really controlling it, which is embarrassing to say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to make something right or fix something when you repeat these behaviors or make these patterns?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’m trying to make something right or fix it. That’s what people keep asking me, like if I’m trying to correct the boxes or make some signal mean something, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nAt the warehouse, I guess that’s why they brought me in, because I keep doing these things that don’t make sense to everyone else. Like I’ll be near the forklift and just kind of freeze with my arms up, or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds it makes. If someone is doing hand signals, I might copy those too, and then it turns into this whole thing where people are looking at me like, “What are you doing?”\n\nAnd with the boxes, I’m not thinking, “This is wrong, I have to fix it.” I’ll just start moving them into these strange little patterns or lines, and even when everyone is yelling for me to stop because I’m holding up the work, I don’t stop right away. I hear them, but it’s like I’m already caught in it.\n\nSo, no, not really fixing. It’s more like the behavior grabs me before I’ve really decided anything.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Like I’ll be by the forklift and just sort of freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping sounds it makes, or copy the hand signals people are doing. It’s more like once it starts happening, I have a hard time breaking out of it, even when I know people are getting mad.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset when you're not allowed to finish one of your patterns or behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do get upset, but it’s not always like I’m mad because I “wanted to finish” it, like it was a project or something. It’s more like when somebody interrupts me, especially at work, it jolts me and makes everything worse. Like if I’m moving the boxes into those little patterns and someone starts yelling, “Stop doing that, get back to work,” I know they’re probably right, but in the moment it feels like my body doesn’t switch over fast enough.\n\nAnd then I get embarrassed, because everyone is looking at me, and then I’m more tense, and then sometimes I get more stuck instead of less. Same with the forklift stuff. I’ll be standing there with my arms up, kind of frozen, or I’ll catch myself copying the beeping sounds or the hand signals, and if someone cuts in and yells at me, I feel… I don’t know, cornered maybe. Not exactly angry at them, just upset and confused and like I can’t get myself to stop quickly enough.\n\nSo, yeah, I guess I do get upset when I’m not allowed to keep going, but it’s not because the pattern is important in a normal way. It’s more that stopping suddenly feels really hard, and then everybody yelling makes it feel even harder.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do things in a specific way or follow a certain routine in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a routine, like a normal routine where I have to brush my teeth a certain way or line things up before bed or anything like that. It’s not really my whole day being scheduled or me having rules for everything.\n\nIt’s more like… in the moment, especially at the warehouse, I get caught doing things in this certain way and I can’t shift out of it fast enough. Like I’ve said, I’ll be by the forklift and suddenly I’m just standing there with my arms up, kind of frozen, or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds it makes, or copying the hand signals people are doing. And with the boxes, instead of just moving them where they’re supposed to go, I’ll start arranging them into these odd little patterns or lines, even when everyone’s telling me to stop.\n\nSo it’s not like I wake up and think, “I have to follow this routine today.” It’s more like something grabs my attention and then I start doing it the same way over and over, and people get mad because I’m supposed to be working, not doing all that. I know it looks weird. I can tell from how they react. But when it’s happening, it feels really hard to just drop it and act normal right away.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these repetitive behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel like they're happening to you against your will?\n\nPatient: I don’t really feel like it’s “me,” if that makes sense. I mean, I know I’m the one doing it, and I can see my hands moving the boxes or hear myself copying the forklift beeps, so I’m not saying someone else is doing it. But it doesn’t feel like a choice the way normal choices feel.\n\nLike at the warehouse, I’ll be near the forklift and then I’m just… stuck there with my arms up, or I start copying the hand signals people are doing, or making the same beeping sound back. And with the boxes, I’m supposed to be moving them for work, but then I start putting them into these weird little lines or patterns instead. People are yelling at me to stop, and part of me knows I’m making everything worse, but I can’t just snap out of it fast enough.\n\nSo I guess it feels more like it’s happening to me, or like I get pulled into it. It’s embarrassing because it looks like I’m trying to be difficult, but I’m really not trying to make everyone mad. It just kind of takes over in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your behaviors have caused problems for you in your relationships or daily life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s caused problems. Not just at work, although work is the big one people notice first. I lost my forklift duties, which is… honestly that was a big deal, because that was one of the things I was trusted to do. Now they don’t want me around it the same way because they say it’s not safe, or they can’t count on me to keep moving and not get stuck doing something else. And I get why they say that, but it still feels bad, like I got pushed down to being less useful.\n\nOutside of work, it’s been happening too. I’ve fallen behind in my community-college classes because I’ll miss things or not keep up right, and once you fall behind it’s like every assignment makes the next one harder. I used to think I could catch up if I just had a quiet weekend or something, but then I don’t, and then people start asking why I’m not turning stuff in or why I’m not there.\n\nAnd at church, I stopped showing up for the food-bank shifts. I wasn’t fired or anything, not exactly, but it got to where I could tell people didn’t really trust me to be dependable. They’d say things like they need to know who’s actually going to be there, or they need things to be safe and organized, and I felt like they were saying it carefully because they didn’t want to be mean. So I just stopped going, because it was easier than showing up and feeling like everyone was watching to see if I messed something up.\n\nSo, yeah, it’s not just annoying habits or whatever. It’s messed with work, school, and even the things I was trying to do to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you repeat or the patterns you make have to be symmetrical or perfect in some way?\n\nPatient: Not really symmetrical, no. It’s not like I’m measuring the boxes or trying to make both sides match, like some perfect shape. I don’t look at it and think, “That corner is wrong” or “This has to be even.” It’s more… I just start moving them into these strange little patterns or lines, and then I keep going with it even when I’m supposed to stop.\n\nLike at the warehouse, I’ll be near the forklift and I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping sounds it makes, or copy hand signals someone is doing, and it’s the same kind of thing with the boxes. Something about the way they’re sitting there, or the movement, I guess, catches me, and then I’m arranging them instead of doing the actual job. People can be yelling at me to cut it out, and I still don’t switch back right away.\n\nSo, no, I wouldn’t say it has to be perfect. It’s not about making it beautiful or balanced. It’s just these odd patterns I get pulled into, and then everybody sees it and gets upset because I’m holding things up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you repeat or the patterns you make are driven by a need to make things orderly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s exactly about order, like not in the way people mean when they say they need things neat. I’m not trying to clean the warehouse up or make the boxes make sense. It’s more like I get caught in doing the thing.\n\nLike with the boxes, I’ll start moving them into these weird little lines or shapes, and I know I’m supposed to be doing the actual job, getting them where they’re supposed to go, but then I’m just… doing that instead. And people will be yelling, like, “Stop, leave them alone,” or telling me I’m holding things up, and I can hear them, but it takes me a while to break out of it.\n\nSame with the forklift stuff. I’ll be standing there with my arms up, or copying the beeping sounds, or copying somebody’s hand signals, and it’s not because I’m trying to make things orderly. It’s more like the movement or the sound grabs me and I start repeating it. I guess that’s why they brought me in, because it keeps happening and it looks strange and it causes problems, but in my head it doesn’t feel like, “I need this to be organized.” It feels more like I’m stuck in the behavior.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to control something when you repeat these behaviors or make these patterns?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m trying to control something, exactly. That’s the part that’s hard to explain, because people see me doing it and they think I must have some reason, like I’m trying to make the warehouse safer or fix the boxes or signal something, but it doesn’t feel like that inside.\n\nIt’s more like… I get pulled into it. Like by the forklift, I’ll just freeze with my arms up, and I know that looks ridiculous or maybe even dangerous, but in the moment I’m just there like that. Or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds, or if somebody is doing hand signals, I’ll copy those too, and it’s not like I’m thinking, “If I do this, then something will be under control.” I’m not making a plan. It just starts happening, and then once I’m in it, stopping feels slow, like my body didn’t get the message yet.\n\nSame with the boxes. I’ll start moving them into these strange little patterns, and everyone is yelling at me to stop because I’m holding everything up, and I can hear them, but it’s like I can’t switch gears right away. I’m not trying to control the boxes or control other people. If anything, it feels like I’m the one not really controlling it, which is embarrassing to say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to make something right or fix something when you repeat these behaviors or make these patterns?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’m trying to make something right or fix it. That’s what people keep asking me, like if I’m trying to correct the boxes or make some signal mean something, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nAt the warehouse, I guess that’s why they brought me in, because I keep doing these things that don’t make sense to everyone else. Like I’ll be near the forklift and just kind of freeze with my arms up, or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds it makes. If someone is doing hand signals, I might copy those too, and then it turns into this whole thing where people are looking at me like, “What are you doing?”\n\nAnd with the boxes, I’m not thinking, “This is wrong, I have to fix it.” I’ll just start moving them into these strange little patterns or lines, and even when everyone is yelling for me to stop because I’m holding up the work, I don’t stop right away. I hear them, but it’s like I’m already caught in it.\n\nSo, no, not really fixing. It’s more like the behavior grabs me before I’ve really decided anything.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if “have to” or “want to” is exactly right. It doesn’t feel like wanting to, like I’m choosing it because it’s enjoyable or because I think it’s a good idea. I mean, I don’t want everyone staring at me or yelling at me at work. I don’t want to be the guy holding things up.

But it also doesn’t feel like a normal “I have to” thing, like there’s a clear rule in my head. It’s more like I just get pulled into it before I’ve really decided anything. Like at the warehouse, I’ll be by the forklift and suddenly I’m standing there with my arms up, or I’m copying the beeping sounds it makes, or copying the hand signals someone else is doing. And then it keeps going longer than it should.

With the boxes, same thing. I’ll start moving them into these strange little patterns, and I know people are getting mad, I can hear them telling me to stop, but I don’t stop right away. It’s not because I want to make trouble. It’s more like my body is already doing it and my mind is lagging behind trying to catch up.

Like everything is crooked or unfinished, and I keep wanting to go back and fix it.\n\nThat’s part of why they brought me in, I think. At the warehouse I’ll be by the forklift and just freeze there with my arms up, or I’ll start making the beeping sound back at it, or copying the signals the guys are doing. And with the boxes, I’ll move them into these little patterns or lines, even when people are yelling, like, “Stop, those don’t go there,” or “Get out of the way.” I hear them, but I’m still trying to make it come out right, and then everybody gets upset.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing these kinds of repetitive behaviors, like making patterns or copying sounds, when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Um… I mean, the main stuff people are upset about is at work. That’s where it’s really obvious, I guess, because there’s the forklift and the boxes and everybody’s trying to move fast, so when I do it there, it becomes a problem right away.\n\nLike I said, I’ll be near the forklift and just kind of stop with my arms up, like I’m stuck there, even though I know I shouldn’t be standing there. Or I’ll copy the beeping sounds it makes, or the hand signals the guys use, and I’ll do it more than once, not just like a joke. And the boxes… I’ll start moving them into these little patterns or lines instead of where they’re supposed to go, even while people are yelling at me to stop or saying I’m messing everything up.\n\nOutside of work, I don’t know… I’m not around forklifts or pallets of boxes the same way, so it doesn’t come up like that. But the reason I’m here is mostly because at the warehouse it keeps happening and they’re saying I can’t keep doing it there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to understand or talk to your coworkers when you're not doing these repetitive behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I’m not doing the box thing or the beeping thing, talking to them can get messed up. Like my supervisor will ask something simple, like which pallets are supposed to go on the truck, and I’ll try to answer, I really will, because I can see the load sheet and I know he needs the answer fast.\n\nBut then when I start explaining it, my words don’t stay on the pallets. I’ll be saying, “That one goes with the load sheet,” and then somehow I’m talking about my old house, because the paper reminds me of boxes there, or moving, and then I’ll jump to fishing, because there were coolers and crates, and then I’ll be saying something about church boxes or donation boxes, and I can tell by their faces they’re not following me anymore.\n\nIt’s frustrating because in my head it feels connected when it starts, like there’s a reason I’m saying it, but by the time I’m done, nobody knows which pallets I meant. Then they get irritated and ask me again, shorter, like “Left or right?” and I feel embarrassed because I was trying to answer the first time.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty making or keeping friends outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. I mean, it’s not like I have a big group of friends anyway, but it’s gotten harder to keep people around outside of work too.\n\nI used to have things where I’d see people, like at the church food-bank shifts, and that kind of counted for me, because you’re working next to people and talking a little, and after a while they know you. But I stopped showing up there because people started saying they couldn’t count on me, or that they didn’t feel like things were safe around me anymore. That really stung, because I wasn’t trying to be unreliable or dangerous or whatever. I just… I guess I wasn’t doing what they needed me to do.\n\nAnd at work, I’ve already lost the forklift duties, so that changed how people treat me too. Some guys used to talk to me more when I was doing that job, but now it’s like they don’t want to be put with me, or they keep checking on me like I’m going to mess something up. Then with school, I’ve fallen behind in my community-college classes, so I’m not really keeping up with people there either. I’ll miss things or be behind, and then it’s awkward to talk to classmates because they’re moving on and I’m still trying to catch up.\n\nSo, yeah… I don’t know if it’s “making friends” exactly, but keeping connections has gotten pretty bad. People don’t seem to trust me the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or distressed when someone stops you from making the patterns with the boxes or copying the sounds?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I do get upset, but it’s not always like I’m angry at them exactly. It’s more like everything gets interrupted and suddenly everybody’s looking at me, and I realize I’m doing it again.\n\nLike at the warehouse, if I’m making the boxes into those little lines or patterns, I can feel like I’m just trying to get them to sit right, like there’s a way they’re supposed to be arranged. And then someone yells, “Stop doing that,” or they grab a box back, and it kind of jars me. Same with the forklift stuff — I’ll freeze with my arms up near it, or start copying the beeping or the hand signals, and when someone cuts me off or tells me to knock it off, I feel embarrassed and tense.\n\nSometimes I want to explain that I’m not trying to mess everything up, but then it’s already a big scene because people are shouting and the work is stopped. So yeah, I feel distressed, mostly because I know they’re mad and I know I’m causing problems, but in the moment it’s hard to just… stop cleanly and act normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating any specific actions or behaviors when you're alone or not at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, sort of. It’s not only at work, but work is where people notice it the most because it slows everybody down and there are forklifts and boxes everywhere.\n\nLike, the reason they even made me come talk to somebody, I think, is because at the warehouse I keep doing these things that don’t really make sense to other people. I’ll freeze with my arms up near the forklift, like I’m stuck in the middle of a motion, or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds or the hand signals. And with the boxes, I’ll start moving them into these little patterns, like they need to line up a certain way, even if that’s not what I’m supposed to be doing.\n\nWhen I’m by myself, it can be more quiet, I guess. There’s nobody yelling, so it doesn’t turn into such a big thing. But I still notice myself wanting to repeat the same kind of movements or arrange things in a certain pattern. It’s hard to explain because it doesn’t feel like I’m deciding, “Okay, now I’m going to do this weird thing.” It just starts happening, and then I realize I’ve been doing it for a while. At work it gets worse because everyone’s shouting for me to stop, and that makes me more tense and embarrassed, and then I feel even more stuck.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the patterns you make with the boxes or the sounds you copy need to be symmetrical or perfect in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “symmetrical” is exactly the word I’d use. It’s more like… they look wrong if they’re just sitting there random, and then I start moving them into these little lines or shapes, like they’re supposed to go a certain way. I’m not standing there thinking, “This has to be perfect,” like a math thing. It’s more that I get caught in it.\n\nThat’s kind of why they brought me in, I guess. At the warehouse I’ll be near the forklift and I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping sounds or the hand signals, and with the boxes I’ll move them into strange little patterns even when people are yelling at me to stop. By then it’s not really about making something beautiful or perfectly even. It’s just like I’m stuck doing it, and everyone else is getting mad because I’m not doing the job right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the patterns you make or the sounds you copy have to be exactly right in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “exactly right” like I’m checking it against something, you know? It’s not like I have a picture in my head and I’m trying to match it perfectly. It’s more like I get pulled into it, and if it’s off, or if the boxes are just sitting there in a way that feels wrong, I start moving them around into these little patterns.\n\nThat’s kind of the whole problem at work. I guess they brought me in because at the warehouse I keep doing stuff that people say is weird or disruptive. Like I’ll be by the forklift and just sort of freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping sounds it makes, or copy the hand signals people are doing. And with the boxes, I’ll start making these strange little lines or patterns with them, even when everyone is yelling at me to stop.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d say it has to be perfect, exactly. It’s more like once it starts happening, I have a hard time breaking out of it, even when I know people are getting mad.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset when you're not allowed to finish one of your patterns or behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do get upset, but it’s not always like I’m mad because I “wanted to finish” it, like it was a project or something. It’s more like when somebody interrupts me, especially at work, it jolts me and makes everything worse. Like if I’m moving the boxes into those little patterns and someone starts yelling, “Stop doing that, get back to work,” I know they’re probably right, but in the moment it feels like my body doesn’t switch over fast enough.\n\nAnd then I get embarrassed, because everyone is looking at me, and then I’m more tense, and then sometimes I get more stuck instead of less. Same with the forklift stuff. I’ll be standing there with my arms up, kind of frozen, or I’ll catch myself copying the beeping sounds or the hand signals, and if someone cuts in and yells at me, I feel… I don’t know, cornered maybe. Not exactly angry at them, just upset and confused and like I can’t get myself to stop quickly enough.\n\nSo, yeah, I guess I do get upset when I’m not allowed to keep going, but it’s not because the pattern is important in a normal way. It’s more that stopping suddenly feels really hard, and then everybody yelling makes it feel even harder.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do things in a specific way or follow a certain routine in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a routine, like a normal routine where I have to brush my teeth a certain way or line things up before bed or anything like that. It’s not really my whole day being scheduled or me having rules for everything.\n\nIt’s more like… in the moment, especially at the warehouse, I get caught doing things in this certain way and I can’t shift out of it fast enough. Like I’ve said, I’ll be by the forklift and suddenly I’m just standing there with my arms up, kind of frozen, or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds it makes, or copying the hand signals people are doing. And with the boxes, instead of just moving them where they’re supposed to go, I’ll start arranging them into these odd little patterns or lines, even when everyone’s telling me to stop.\n\nSo it’s not like I wake up and think, “I have to follow this routine today.” It’s more like something grabs my attention and then I start doing it the same way over and over, and people get mad because I’m supposed to be working, not doing all that. I know it looks weird. I can tell from how they react. But when it’s happening, it feels really hard to just drop it and act normal right away.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these repetitive behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel like they're happening to you against your will?\n\nPatient: I don’t really feel like it’s “me,” if that makes sense. I mean, I know I’m the one doing it, and I can see my hands moving the boxes or hear myself copying the forklift beeps, so I’m not saying someone else is doing it. But it doesn’t feel like a choice the way normal choices feel.\n\nLike at the warehouse, I’ll be near the forklift and then I’m just… stuck there with my arms up, or I start copying the hand signals people are doing, or making the same beeping sound back. And with the boxes, I’m supposed to be moving them for work, but then I start putting them into these weird little lines or patterns instead. People are yelling at me to stop, and part of me knows I’m making everything worse, but I can’t just snap out of it fast enough.\n\nSo I guess it feels more like it’s happening to me, or like I get pulled into it. It’s embarrassing because it looks like I’m trying to be difficult, but I’m really not trying to make everyone mad. It just kind of takes over in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your behaviors have caused problems for you in your relationships or daily life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s caused problems. Not just at work, although work is the big one people notice first. I lost my forklift duties, which is… honestly that was a big deal, because that was one of the things I was trusted to do. Now they don’t want me around it the same way because they say it’s not safe, or they can’t count on me to keep moving and not get stuck doing something else. And I get why they say that, but it still feels bad, like I got pushed down to being less useful.\n\nOutside of work, it’s been happening too. I’ve fallen behind in my community-college classes because I’ll miss things or not keep up right, and once you fall behind it’s like every assignment makes the next one harder. I used to think I could catch up if I just had a quiet weekend or something, but then I don’t, and then people start asking why I’m not turning stuff in or why I’m not there.\n\nAnd at church, I stopped showing up for the food-bank shifts. I wasn’t fired or anything, not exactly, but it got to where I could tell people didn’t really trust me to be dependable. They’d say things like they need to know who’s actually going to be there, or they need things to be safe and organized, and I felt like they were saying it carefully because they didn’t want to be mean. So I just stopped going, because it was easier than showing up and feeling like everyone was watching to see if I messed something up.\n\nSo, yeah, it’s not just annoying habits or whatever. It’s messed with work, school, and even the things I was trying to do to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you repeat or the patterns you make have to be symmetrical or perfect in some way?\n\nPatient: Not really symmetrical, no. It’s not like I’m measuring the boxes or trying to make both sides match, like some perfect shape. I don’t look at it and think, “That corner is wrong” or “This has to be even.” It’s more… I just start moving them into these strange little patterns or lines, and then I keep going with it even when I’m supposed to stop.\n\nLike at the warehouse, I’ll be near the forklift and I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping sounds it makes, or copy hand signals someone is doing, and it’s the same kind of thing with the boxes. Something about the way they’re sitting there, or the movement, I guess, catches me, and then I’m arranging them instead of doing the actual job. People can be yelling at me to cut it out, and I still don’t switch back right away.\n\nSo, no, I wouldn’t say it has to be perfect. It’s not about making it beautiful or balanced. It’s just these odd patterns I get pulled into, and then everybody sees it and gets upset because I’m holding things up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you repeat or the patterns you make are driven by a need to make things orderly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s exactly about order, like not in the way people mean when they say they need things neat. I’m not trying to clean the warehouse up or make the boxes make sense. It’s more like I get caught in doing the thing.\n\nLike with the boxes, I’ll start moving them into these weird little lines or shapes, and I know I’m supposed to be doing the actual job, getting them where they’re supposed to go, but then I’m just… doing that instead. And people will be yelling, like, “Stop, leave them alone,” or telling me I’m holding things up, and I can hear them, but it takes me a while to break out of it.\n\nSame with the forklift stuff. I’ll be standing there with my arms up, or copying the beeping sounds, or copying somebody’s hand signals, and it’s not because I’m trying to make things orderly. It’s more like the movement or the sound grabs me and I start repeating it. I guess that’s why they brought me in, because it keeps happening and it looks strange and it causes problems, but in my head it doesn’t feel like, “I need this to be organized.” It feels more like I’m stuck in the behavior.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to control something when you repeat these behaviors or make these patterns?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m trying to control something, exactly. That’s the part that’s hard to explain, because people see me doing it and they think I must have some reason, like I’m trying to make the warehouse safer or fix the boxes or signal something, but it doesn’t feel like that inside.\n\nIt’s more like… I get pulled into it. Like by the forklift, I’ll just freeze with my arms up, and I know that looks ridiculous or maybe even dangerous, but in the moment I’m just there like that. Or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds, or if somebody is doing hand signals, I’ll copy those too, and it’s not like I’m thinking, “If I do this, then something will be under control.” I’m not making a plan. It just starts happening, and then once I’m in it, stopping feels slow, like my body didn’t get the message yet.\n\nSame with the boxes. I’ll start moving them into these strange little patterns, and everyone is yelling at me to stop because I’m holding everything up, and I can hear them, but it’s like I can’t switch gears right away. I’m not trying to control the boxes or control other people. If anything, it feels like I’m the one not really controlling it, which is embarrassing to say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to make something right or fix something when you repeat these behaviors or make these patterns?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’m trying to make something right or fix it. That’s what people keep asking me, like if I’m trying to correct the boxes or make some signal mean something, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nAt the warehouse, I guess that’s why they brought me in, because I keep doing these things that don’t make sense to everyone else. Like I’ll be near the forklift and just kind of freeze with my arms up, or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds it makes. If someone is doing hand signals, I might copy those too, and then it turns into this whole thing where people are looking at me like, “What are you doing?”\n\nAnd with the boxes, I’m not thinking, “This is wrong, I have to fix it.” I’ll just start moving them into these strange little patterns or lines, and even when everyone is yelling for me to stop because I’m holding up the work, I don’t stop right away. I hear them, but it’s like I’m already caught in it.\n\nSo, no, not really fixing. It’s more like the behavior grabs me before I’ve really decided anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these things because you feel like you have to, or because you want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “have to” or “want to” is exactly right. It doesn’t feel like wanting to, like I’m choosing it because it’s enjoyable or because I think it’s a good idea. I mean, I don’t want everyone staring at me or yelling at me at work. I don’t want to be the guy holding things up.\n\nBut it also doesn’t feel like a normal “I have to” thing, like there’s a clear rule in my head. It’s more like I just get pulled into it before I’ve really decided anything. I’ll start moving them into these strange little patterns, and I know people are getting mad, I can hear them telling me to stop, but I don’t stop right away. It’s not because I want to make trouble. It’s more like my body is already doing it and my mind is lagging behind trying to catch up.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. There’s nobody yelling, so it doesn’t turn into such a big thing. But I still notice myself wanting to repeat the same kind of movements or arrange things in a certain pattern. It’s hard to explain because it doesn’t feel like I’m deciding, “Okay, now I’m going to do this weird thing.” It just starts happening, and then I realize I’ve been doing it for a while. At work it gets worse because everyone’s shouting for me to stop, and that makes me more tense and embarrassed, and then I feel even more stuck.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the patterns you make with the boxes or the sounds you copy need to be symmetrical or perfect in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “symmetrical” is exactly the word I’d use. It’s more like… they look wrong if they’re just sitting there random, and then I start moving them into these little lines or shapes, like they’re supposed to go a certain way. I’m not standing there thinking, “This has to be perfect,” like a math thing. It’s more that I get caught in it.\n\nThat’s kind of why they brought me in, I guess. At the warehouse I’ll be near the forklift and I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping sounds or the hand signals, and with the boxes I’ll move them into strange little patterns even when people are yelling at me to stop. By then it’s not really about making something beautiful or perfectly even. It’s just like I’m stuck doing it, and everyone else is getting mad because I’m not doing the job right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the patterns you make or the sounds you copy have to be exactly right in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “exactly right” like I’m checking it against something, you know? It’s not like I have a picture in my head and I’m trying to match it perfectly. It’s more like I get pulled into it, and if it’s off, or if the boxes are just sitting there in a way that feels wrong, I start moving them around into these little patterns.\n\nThat’s kind of the whole problem at work. I guess they brought me in because at the warehouse I keep doing stuff that people say is weird or disruptive. Like I’ll be by the forklift and just sort of freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping sounds it makes, or copy the hand signals people are doing. And with the boxes, I’ll start making these strange little lines or patterns with them, even when everyone is yelling at me to stop.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d say it has to be perfect, exactly. It’s more like once it starts happening, I have a hard time breaking out of it, even when I know people are getting mad.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset when you're not allowed to finish one of your patterns or behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do get upset, but it’s not always like I’m mad because I “wanted to finish” it, like it was a project or something. It’s more like when somebody interrupts me, especially at work, it jolts me and makes everything worse. Like if I’m moving the boxes into those little patterns and someone starts yelling, “Stop doing that, get back to work,” I know they’re probably right, but in the moment it feels like my body doesn’t switch over fast enough.\n\nAnd then I get embarrassed, because everyone is looking at me, and then I’m more tense, and then sometimes I get more stuck instead of less. Same with the forklift stuff. I’ll be standing there with my arms up, kind of frozen, or I’ll catch myself copying the beeping sounds or the hand signals, and if someone cuts in and yells at me, I feel… I don’t know, cornered maybe. Not exactly angry at them, just upset and confused and like I can’t get myself to stop quickly enough.\n\nSo, yeah, I guess I do get upset when I’m not allowed to keep going, but it’s not because the pattern is important in a normal way. It’s more that stopping suddenly feels really hard, and then everybody yelling makes it feel even harder.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do things in a specific way or follow a certain routine in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a routine, like a normal routine where I have to brush my teeth a certain way or line things up before bed or anything like that. It’s not really my whole day being scheduled or me having rules for everything.\n\nIt’s more like… in the moment, especially at the warehouse, I get caught doing things in this certain way and I can’t shift out of it fast enough. Like I’ve said, I’ll be by the forklift and suddenly I’m just standing there with my arms up, kind of frozen, or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds it makes, or copying the hand signals people are doing. And with the boxes, instead of just moving them where they’re supposed to go, I’ll start arranging them into these odd little patterns or lines, even when everyone’s telling me to stop.\n\nSo it’s not like I wake up and think, “I have to follow this routine today.” It’s more like something grabs my attention and then I start doing it the same way over and over, and people get mad because I’m supposed to be working, not doing all that. I know it looks weird. I can tell from how they react. But when it’s happening, it feels really hard to just drop it and act normal right away.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these repetitive behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel like they're happening to you against your will?\n\nPatient: I don’t really feel like it’s “me,” if that makes sense. I mean, I know I’m the one doing it, and I can see my hands moving the boxes or hear myself copying the forklift beeps, so I’m not saying someone else is doing it. But it doesn’t feel like a choice the way normal choices feel.\n\nLike at the warehouse, I’ll be near the forklift and then I’m just… stuck there with my arms up, or I start copying the hand signals people are doing, or making the same beeping sound back. And with the boxes, I’m supposed to be moving them for work, but then I start putting them into these weird little lines or patterns instead. People are yelling at me to stop, and part of me knows I’m making everything worse, but I can’t just snap out of it fast enough.\n\nSo I guess it feels more like it’s happening to me, or like I get pulled into it. It’s embarrassing because it looks like I’m trying to be difficult, but I’m really not trying to make everyone mad. It just kind of takes over in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your behaviors have caused problems for you in your relationships or daily life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s caused problems. Not just at work, although work is the big one people notice first. I lost my forklift duties, which is… honestly that was a big deal, because that was one of the things I was trusted to do. Now they don’t want me around it the same way because they say it’s not safe, or they can’t count on me to keep moving and not get stuck doing something else. And I get why they say that, but it still feels bad, like I got pushed down to being less useful.\n\nOutside of work, it’s been happening too. I’ve fallen behind in my community-college classes because I’ll miss things or not keep up right, and once you fall behind it’s like every assignment makes the next one harder. I used to think I could catch up if I just had a quiet weekend or something, but then I don’t, and then people start asking why I’m not turning stuff in or why I’m not there.\n\nAnd at church, I stopped showing up for the food-bank shifts. I wasn’t fired or anything, not exactly, but it got to where I could tell people didn’t really trust me to be dependable. They’d say things like they need to know who’s actually going to be there, or they need things to be safe and organized, and I felt like they were saying it carefully because they didn’t want to be mean. So I just stopped going, because it was easier than showing up and feeling like everyone was watching to see if I messed something up.\n\nSo, yeah, it’s not just annoying habits or whatever. It’s messed with work, school, and even the things I was trying to do to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you repeat or the patterns you make have to be symmetrical or perfect in some way?\n\nPatient: Not really symmetrical, no. It’s not like I’m measuring the boxes or trying to make both sides match, like some perfect shape. I don’t look at it and think, “That corner is wrong” or “This has to be even.” It’s more… I just start moving them into these strange little patterns or lines, and then I keep going with it even when I’m supposed to stop.\n\nLike at the warehouse, I’ll be near the forklift and I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping sounds it makes, or copy hand signals someone is doing, and it’s the same kind of thing with the boxes. Something about the way they’re sitting there, or the movement, I guess, catches me, and then I’m arranging them instead of doing the actual job. People can be yelling at me to cut it out, and I still don’t switch back right away.\n\nSo, no, I wouldn’t say it has to be perfect. It’s not about making it beautiful or balanced. It’s just these odd patterns I get pulled into, and then everybody sees it and gets upset because I’m holding things up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you repeat or the patterns you make are driven by a need to make things orderly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s exactly about order, like not in the way people mean when they say they need things neat. I’m not trying to clean the warehouse up or make the boxes make sense. It’s more like I get caught in doing the thing.\n\nLike with the boxes, I’ll start moving them into these weird little lines or shapes, and I know I’m supposed to be doing the actual job, getting them where they’re supposed to go, but then I’m just… doing that instead. And people will be yelling, like, “Stop, leave them alone,” or telling me I’m holding things up, and I can hear them, but it takes me a while to break out of it.\n\nSame with the forklift stuff. I’ll be standing there with my arms up, or copying the beeping sounds, or copying somebody’s hand signals, and it’s not because I’m trying to make things orderly. It’s more like the movement or the sound grabs me and I start repeating it. I guess that’s why they brought me in, because it keeps happening and it looks strange and it causes problems, but in my head it doesn’t feel like, “I need this to be organized.” It feels more like I’m stuck in the behavior.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to control something when you repeat these behaviors or make these patterns?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m trying to control something, exactly. That’s the part that’s hard to explain, because people see me doing it and they think I must have some reason, like I’m trying to make the warehouse safer or fix the boxes or signal something, but it doesn’t feel like that inside.\n\nIt’s more like… I get pulled into it. Like by the forklift, I’ll just freeze with my arms up, and I know that looks ridiculous or maybe even dangerous, but in the moment I’m just there like that. Or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds, or if somebody is doing hand signals, I’ll copy those too, and it’s not like I’m thinking, “If I do this, then something will be under control.” I’m not making a plan. It just starts happening, and then once I’m in it, stopping feels slow, like my body didn’t get the message yet.\n\nSame with the boxes. I’ll start moving them into these strange little patterns, and everyone is yelling at me to stop because I’m holding everything up, and I can hear them, but it’s like I can’t switch gears right away. I’m not trying to control the boxes or control other people. If anything, it feels like I’m the one not really controlling it, which is embarrassing to say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to make something right or fix something when you repeat these behaviors or make these patterns?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’m trying to make something right or fix it. I hear them, but it’s like I’m already caught in it.\n\nSo, no, not really fixing. It’s more like the behavior grabs me before I’ve really decided anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these things because you feel like you have to, or because you want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “have to” or “want to” is exactly right. It’s more like I just get pulled into it before I’ve really decided anything. Not really in the way you’re saying it, I don’t think. It’s not like I need everything in a certain order so I can relax, like the boxes have to be lined up perfectly or something bad happens if they aren’t. I don’t have some exact order in my head that makes me comfortable.

It’s more that I just start doing it, and then it becomes hard to stop. Like I guess they brought me in because at the warehouse I keep doing these things that look like they must mean something. I’ll be near the forklift and I’ll just freeze with my arms up, or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds, and if someone is doing hand signals I might copy those too. And then with the boxes, I’ll move them into these weird little patterns, but I’m not thinking, “This is the right order” or “Now I’ll feel better.”

People are yelling at me to stop because I’m slowing everything down, and I hear them, but it’s like I’m stuck in the action for a while. So I wouldn’t say things need to happen a certain way for me to feel comfortable. It’s more like the thing happens and I get caught in it.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I guess I’m here because of work. I don’t always mean to make it a big thing, it just happens and then everyone gets mad.\n\nAnd with the boxes, I’ve been moving them into these little patterns instead of where they’re supposed to go. I’ll be saying, “That one goes with the load sheet,” and then somehow I’m talking about my old house, because the paper reminds me of boxes there, or moving, and then I’ll jump to fishing, because there were coolers and crates, and then I’ll be saying something about church boxes or donation boxes, and I can tell by their faces they’re not following me anymore.\n\nIt’s frustrating because in my head it feels connected when it starts, like there’s a reason I’m saying it, but by the time I’m done, nobody knows which pallets I meant. Then they get irritated and ask me again, shorter, like “Left or right?” and I feel embarrassed because I was trying to answer the first time.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty making or keeping friends outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. I mean, it’s not like I have a big group of friends anyway, but it’s gotten harder to keep people around outside of work too.\n\nI used to have things where I’d see people, like at the church food-bank shifts, and that kind of counted for me, because you’re working next to people and talking a little, and after a while they know you. But I stopped showing up there because people started saying they couldn’t count on me, or that they didn’t feel like things were safe around me anymore. That really stung, because I wasn’t trying to be unreliable or dangerous or whatever. I just… I guess I wasn’t doing what they needed me to do.\n\nAnd at work, I’ve already lost the forklift duties, so that changed how people treat me too. Some guys used to talk to me more when I was doing that job, but now it’s like they don’t want to be put with me, or they keep checking on me like I’m going to mess something up. Then with school, I’ve fallen behind in my community-college classes, so I’m not really keeping up with people there either. I’ll miss things or be behind, and then it’s awkward to talk to classmates because they’re moving on and I’m still trying to catch up.\n\nSo, yeah… I don’t know if it’s “making friends” exactly, but keeping connections has gotten pretty bad. People don’t seem to trust me the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or distressed when someone stops you from making the patterns with the boxes or copying the sounds?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I do get upset, but it’s not always like I’m angry at them exactly. It’s more like everything gets interrupted and suddenly everybody’s looking at me, and I realize I’m doing it again.\n\nLike at the warehouse, if I’m making the boxes into those little lines or patterns, I can feel like I’m just trying to get them to sit right, like there’s a way they’re supposed to be arranged. And then someone yells, “Stop doing that,” or they grab a box back, and it kind of jars me. Same with the forklift stuff — I’ll freeze with my arms up near it, or start copying the beeping or the hand signals, and when someone cuts me off or tells me to knock it off, I feel embarrassed and tense.\n\nSometimes I want to explain that I’m not trying to mess everything up, but then it’s already a big scene because people are shouting and the work is stopped. So yeah, I feel distressed, mostly because I know they’re mad and I know I’m causing problems, but in the moment it’s hard to just… stop cleanly and act normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating any specific actions or behaviors when you're alone or not at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, sort of. It’s not only at work, but work is where people notice it the most because it slows everybody down and there are forklifts and boxes everywhere.\n\nLike, the reason they even made me come talk to somebody, I think, is because at the warehouse I keep doing these things that don’t really make sense to other people. I’ll freeze with my arms up near the forklift, like I’m stuck in the middle of a motion, or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds or the hand signals. And with the boxes, I’ll start moving them into these little patterns, like they need to line up a certain way, even if that’s not what I’m supposed to be doing.\n\nWhen I’m by myself, it can be more quiet, I guess. It’s just like I’m stuck doing it, and everyone else is getting mad because I’m not doing the job right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the patterns you make or the sounds you copy have to be exactly right in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “exactly right” like I’m checking it against something, you know? It’s more like when somebody interrupts me, especially at work, it jolts me and makes everything worse. Like if I’m moving the boxes into those little patterns and someone starts yelling, “Stop doing that, get back to work,” I know they’re probably right, but in the moment it feels like my body doesn’t switch over fast enough.\n\nAnd then I get embarrassed, because everyone is looking at me, and then I’m more tense, and then sometimes I get more stuck instead of less. Same with the forklift stuff. Not exactly angry at them, just upset and confused and like I can’t get myself to stop quickly enough.\n\nSo, yeah, I guess I do get upset when I’m not allowed to keep going, but it’s not because the pattern is important in a normal way. It’s more that stopping suddenly feels really hard, and then everybody yelling makes it feel even harder.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do things in a specific way or follow a certain routine in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a routine, like a normal routine where I have to brush my teeth a certain way or line things up before bed or anything like that. It’s not really my whole day being scheduled or me having rules for everything.\n\nIt’s more like… in the moment, especially at the warehouse, I get caught doing things in this certain way and I can’t shift out of it fast enough. Like I’ve said, I’ll be by the forklift and suddenly I’m just standing there with my arms up, kind of frozen, or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds it makes, or copying the hand signals people are doing. I know it looks weird. I can tell from how they react. But when it’s happening, it feels really hard to just drop it and act normal right away.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these repetitive behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel like they're happening to you against your will?\n\nPatient: I don’t really feel like it’s “me,” if that makes sense. I mean, I know I’m the one doing it, and I can see my hands moving the boxes or hear myself copying the forklift beeps, so I’m not saying someone else is doing it. It’s embarrassing because it looks like I’m trying to be difficult, but I’m really not trying to make everyone mad. It just kind of takes over in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your behaviors have caused problems for you in your relationships or daily life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s caused problems. Now they don’t want me around it the same way because they say it’s not safe, or they can’t count on me to keep moving and not get stuck doing something else. I’ve fallen behind in my community-college classes because I’ll miss things or not keep up right, and once you fall behind it’s like every assignment makes the next one harder. I used to think I could catch up if I just had a quiet weekend or something, but then I don’t, and then people start asking why I’m not turning stuff in or why I’m not there.\n\nAnd at church, I stopped showing up for the food-bank shifts. I wasn’t fired or anything, not exactly, but it got to where I could tell people didn’t really trust me to be dependable. They’d say things like they need to know who’s actually going to be there, or they need things to be safe and organized, and I felt like they were saying it carefully because they didn’t want to be mean. So I just stopped going, because it was easier than showing up and feeling like everyone was watching to see if I messed something up.\n\nSo, yeah, it’s not just annoying habits or whatever. It’s messed with work, school, and even the things I was trying to do to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you repeat or the patterns you make have to be symmetrical or perfect in some way?\n\nPatient: Not really symmetrical, no. It’s not like I’m measuring the boxes or trying to make both sides match, like some perfect shape. I don’t look at it and think, “That corner is wrong” or “This has to be even.” It’s more… I just start moving them into these strange little patterns or lines, and then I keep going with it even when I’m supposed to stop.\n\nLike at the warehouse, I’ll be near the forklift and I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping sounds it makes, or copy hand signals someone is doing, and it’s the same kind of thing with the boxes. Something about the way they’re sitting there, or the movement, I guess, catches me, and then I’m arranging them instead of doing the actual job. People can be yelling at me to cut it out, and I still don’t switch back right away.\n\nSo, no, I wouldn’t say it has to be perfect. It’s not about making it beautiful or balanced. It’s just these odd patterns I get pulled into, and then everybody sees it and gets upset because I’m holding things up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you repeat or the patterns you make are driven by a need to make things orderly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s exactly about order, like not in the way people mean when they say they need things neat. I’m not trying to clean the warehouse up or make the boxes make sense. It’s more like I get caught in doing the thing.\n\nLike with the boxes, I’ll start moving them into these weird little lines or shapes, and I know I’m supposed to be doing the actual job, getting them where they’re supposed to go, but then I’m just… doing that instead. And people will be yelling, like, “Stop, leave them alone,” or telling me I’m holding things up, and I can hear them, but it takes me a while to break out of it.\n\nSame with the forklift stuff. I’ll be standing there with my arms up, or copying the beeping sounds, or copying somebody’s hand signals, and it’s not because I’m trying to make things orderly. It’s more like the movement or the sound grabs me and I start repeating it. I guess that’s why they brought me in, because it keeps happening and it looks strange and it causes problems, but in my head it doesn’t feel like, “I need this to be organized.” It feels more like I’m stuck in the behavior.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to control something when you repeat these behaviors or make these patterns?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m trying to control something, exactly. That’s the part that’s hard to explain, because people see me doing it and they think I must have some reason, like I’m trying to make the warehouse safer or fix the boxes or signal something, but it doesn’t feel like that inside.\n\nIt’s more like… I get pulled into it. Like by the forklift, I’ll just freeze with my arms up, and I know that looks ridiculous or maybe even dangerous, but in the moment I’m just there like that. Or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds, or if somebody is doing hand signals, I’ll copy those too, and it’s not like I’m thinking, “If I do this, then something will be under control.” I’m not making a plan. It just starts happening, and then once I’m in it, stopping feels slow, like my body didn’t get the message yet.\n\nSame with the boxes. I’ll start moving them into these strange little patterns, and everyone is yelling at me to stop because I’m holding everything up, and I can hear them, but it’s like I can’t switch gears right away. I’m not trying to control the boxes or control other people. If anything, it feels like I’m the one not really controlling it, which is embarrassing to say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to make something right or fix something when you repeat these behaviors or make these patterns?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’m trying to make something right or fix it. That’s what people keep asking me, like if I’m trying to correct the boxes or make some signal mean something, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nAt the warehouse, I guess that’s why they brought me in, because I keep doing these things that don’t make sense to everyone else. Like I’ll be near the forklift and just kind of freeze with my arms up, or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds it makes. If someone is doing hand signals, I might copy those too, and then it turns into this whole thing where people are looking at me like, “What are you doing?”\n\nAnd with the boxes, I’m not thinking, “This is wrong, I have to fix it.” I’ll just start moving them into these strange little patterns or lines, and even when everyone is yelling for me to stop because I’m holding up the work, I don’t stop right away. I hear them, but it’s like I’m already caught in it.\n\nSo, no, not really fixing. It’s more like the behavior grabs me before I’ve really decided anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these things because you feel like you have to, or because you want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “have to” or “want to” is exactly right. It doesn’t feel like wanting to, like I’m choosing it because it’s enjoyable or because I think it’s a good idea. I mean, I don’t want everyone staring at me or yelling at me at work. I don’t want to be the guy holding things up.\n\nBut it also doesn’t feel like a normal “I have to” thing, like there’s a clear rule in my head. It’s more like I just get pulled into it before I’ve really decided anything. Like at the warehouse, I’ll be by the forklift and suddenly I’m standing there with my arms up, or I’m copying the beeping sounds it makes, or copying the hand signals someone else is doing. And then it keeps going longer than it should.\n\nWith the boxes, same thing. I’ll start moving them into these strange little patterns, and I know people are getting mad, I can hear them telling me to stop, but I don’t stop right away. It’s not because I want to make trouble. It’s more like my body is already doing it and my mind is lagging behind trying to catch up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things need to be a certain way or happen in a certain order for you to feel comfortable?\n\nPatient: Not really in the way you’re saying it, I don’t think. It’s not like I need everything in a certain order so I can relax, like the boxes have to be lined up perfectly or something bad happens if they aren’t. I don’t have some exact order in my head that makes me comfortable.\n\nIt’s more that I just start doing it, and then it becomes hard to stop. Like I guess they brought me in because at the warehouse I keep doing these things that look like they must mean something. I’ll be near the forklift and I’ll just freeze with my arms up, or I’ll start copying the beeping sounds, and if someone is doing hand signals I might copy those too. And then with the boxes, I’ll move them into these weird little patterns, but I’m not thinking, “This is the right order” or “Now I’ll feel better.”\n\nPeople are yelling at me to stop because I’m slowing everything down, and I hear them, but it’s like I’m stuck in the action for a while. So I wouldn’t say things need to happen a certain way for me to feel comfortable. It’s more like the thing happens and I get caught in it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
